Title Path: Broadcast of assistance data > General
Content: Broadcast of positioning assistance data is supported via Positioning System Information Blocks (posSIBs) as specified in TS 36.331 or TS 38.331 . The posSIBs are carried in RRC System Information (SI) messages (TS 36.331 or TS 38.331 ). For LTE RRC System Information (SI), a single SystemInformationBlockPos IE is defined in TS 36.331 which is carried in IE PosSystemInformation-r15-IEs specified in TS 36.331 . The mapping of positioning SIB type (posSibType) to assistance data carried in SystemInformationBlockPos is specified in clause 7.2. For NR RRC System Information (SI), a single SIBpos IE is defined in TS 38.331 which is carried in IE PosSystemInformation-r16-IEs specified in TS 38.331 . The mapping of positioning SIB type (posSibType) to assistance data carried in SIBpos is specified in clause 7.2.
Title Path: Broadcast of assistance data > Mapping of posSibType to assistance data element
Content: The supported posSibType's are specified in Table 7.2-1. The GNSS Common and Generic Assistance Data IEs are defined in clause 6.5.2.2. The OTDOA Assistance Data IEs and NR DL-TDOA/DL-AoD Assistance Data IEs are defined in clause 7.4.2. The Barometric Assistance Data IEs are defined in clause 6.5.5.8. The TBS (based on MBS signals) Assistance Data IEs are defined in clause 6.5.4.8.
Title Path: Broadcast of assistance data > Procedures related to broadcast information elements
Content: Upon receiving AssistanceDataSIBelement, the target device shall: if the segmentationInfo is not included: if the cipheringKeyData is included: if the UE has obtained a valid cipher key value and the first portion of the initial Counter denoted C corresponding to the cipherSetID using NAS signalling: if C contains less than 128-bits: 5 pad out the bit string with zeroes in most significant bit positions to achieve 128 bits. if the d0 field contains less than 128-bits: 5 pad out the bit string with zeroes in least significant bit positions to achieve 128 bits, denoted D . determine the initial Counter C = (C + D ) mod 2128 (where all values are treated as non-negative integers); determine any subsequent counter C from the previous counter C as C = (C + 1) mod 2128; use the sequence of counters C , C , C , and the cipher key value to decipher the assistanceDataElement; decode the deciphered assistanceDataElement and deliver the related assistance data to upper layers. else: 4 discard the AssistanceDataSIBelement. else: 3 decode the assistanceDataElement and deliver the related assistance data to upper layers. else: if segmentationOption indicates 'pseudo-seg': if the cipheringKeyData is included: if the UE has obtained a valid cipher key value and the first portion of the initial Counter denoted C corresponding to the cipherSetID using NAS signalling: if C contains less than 128-bits: 6 pad out the bit string with zeroes in most significant bit positions to achieve 128 bits. if the d0 field contains less than 128-bits: 6 pad out the bit string with zeroes in least significant bit positions to achieve 128 bits, denoted D . determine the initial Counter C = (C + D ) mod 2128 (where all values are treated as non-negative integers); determine any subsequent counter C from the previous counter C as C = (C + 1) mod 2128; use the sequence of counters C , C , C , and the cipher key value to decipher the assistanceDataElement segment; decode the deciphered assistanceDataElement segment and deliver the related assistance data portion together with the assistanceDataSegmentType and assistanceDataSegmentNumber to upper layers. else: 5 discard the AssistanceDataSIBelement segment. else: 4 decode the assistanceDataElement segment and deliver the related assistance data portion together with the assistanceDataSegmentType and assistanceDataSegmentNumber to upper layers. if segmentationOption indicates 'octet-string-seg': 3 if all segments of assistanceDataElement have been received: 4 assemble the assistance data element from the received assistanceDataElement segments; if the cipheringKeyData is included in the first segment: if the UE has obtained a valid cipher key value and the first portion of the initial Counter denoted C corresponding to the cipherSetID using NAS signalling: if C contains less than 128-bits: 8 pad out the bit string with zeroes in most significant bit positions to achieve 128 bits. if the d0 field contains less than 128-bits: 8 pad out the bit string with zeroes in least significant bit positions to achieve 128 bits, denoted D . determine the initial Counter C = (C + D ) mod 2128 (where all values are treated as non-negative integers); determine any subsequent counter C from the previous counter C as C = (C + 1) mod 2128; use the sequence of counters C , C , C , and the cipher key value to decipher the assembled assistance data element; decode the assembled and deciphered assistance data element and deliver the related assistance data to upper layers. else: 7 discard the assembled assistance data element. else: 6 decode the assembled assistance data element and deliver the related assistance data to upper layers. [NOTE:]As an optional optimisation when segmentationOption indicates 'octet-string-seg', a target device may verify if the cipheringKeyData is included in the first segment as soon as the first segment is received and, if included, may verify that the UE has obtained a valid cipher key value and the first portion of the initial Counter denoted C corresponding to the cipherSetID using NAS signalling. When the UE has not obtained a valid cipher key value and initial Counter C using NAS signalling, the UE may discard the first segment and ignore all subsequent segments. The value for D shall be different for different AssistanceDataSIBelement's to ensure that the counters derived from C for any assistanceDataElement are different to the counters for any other assistanceDataElement for a given ciphering key. D shall contain at least 16 least significant bits (LSBs) set to zero to ensure that the values of D differ from another by a large value.

It always contains a multiple of 8 bits. Title Path: Broadcast of assistance data > Broadcast ciphering (informative)
Content: The assistanceDataElement OCTET STRING included in IE AssistanceDataSIBelement may be ciphered using the 128-bit Advanced Encryption Standard (AES) algorithm (with counter mode). AES as specified in and is a block mode cipher algorithm that ciphers blocks of 128 bits at a time. However, Counter mode enables usage for a bit string that is not an exact multiple of 128 bits. Further, Counter mode enables a target (or a server) to perform most of the deciphering (or ciphering) processing independently of receipt of the data to be deciphered (or ciphered) which may enable more efficient processing. Provided counters are chosen in a non repeating manner by the server (which is a requirement for Counter mode), every block of data will be ciphered in a unique manner. The algorithm makes use of a sequence of counters C , C , C , each containing 128 bits, where C is specified by the server and each subsequent counter (C , C etc.) is obtained from the previous counter by adding one modulo 2128. Each counter C is ciphered using the AES algorithm with a common 128-bit key to produce an output block O of 128 bits. To perform ciphering, the assistanceDataElement is divided into blocks B , B , B of 128 bits each, except for the last block B which may contain fewer than 128 bits. The ciphered assistanceDataElement is obtained as a sequence of n blocks containing 128 bits each (except possibly for the last block) given by (O XOR B ), (O XOR B ), (O XOR B ), where XOR denotes bitwise exclusive OR. In the case of the last block, if B contains m bits (m 128), then the m most significant bits of O would be used for the exclusive OR. Deciphering is performed in the same way except that the blocks B , B , B are now obtained from the ciphered message and the result of the exclusive OR operations yields the original unciphered message. Figure 7.5-1 provides an illustration of Counter mode for the generic case of an arbitrary block cipher algorithm CIPH The algorithms require specific conventions for bit ordering. The bit ordering applicable to ciphering for a ASN.1 PER encoded assistanceDataElement is the bit ordering produced by the ASN.1 PER encoding where the first bit is the leading bit number zero, the second bit is bit one etc.. The initial counter C used to cipher an entire assistanceDataElement is provided to a target by a server in two portions. The first portion, denoted C , is provided using point to point mode along with the 128-bit ciphering key and an identifier for both of these values as specified in TS 23.271 . The second portion, denoted D (d0), is provided in unciphered form in IE CipheringKeyData. A target then obtains C as: C = (C + D ) mod 2128 (where all values are treated as non-negative integers) To obtain any subsequent counter C from the previous counter C $ - $ for any message, the following operation is used: []C = (C $ - $ + 1) mod 2128 [NOTE:]As specified in clause 7.3 the value for D is different for different AssistanceDataSIBelement's to ensure that the counters derived from C for any assistanceDataElement can be different to the counters for any other assistanceDataElement. However, a long assistanceDataElement or a segmented assistanceDataElement would require the use of consecutive counter values C to C , where n is the assistanceDataElement size in bits divided by 128 (and rounded up). There would then be a danger of small changes in the value of D for ciphering of different assistanceDataElement's (e.g. D being chosen as 1 larger than a previous D value) reusing previous counter values. To avoid this, the values of D contain 16 least significant bits (LSBs) set to zero, as specified in clause 7.3.
